\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{1}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+i)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{4}+2-i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3}+1-i)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+2-i)}\,dq

as desired.
In the same way we can determine
\widehat{V}_{l_{1}e_{1}+l_{2}e_{2}+l_{3}e_{3}+l_{4}e_{4}+e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

from (
5.2
) with
(i,j,k)=(2,3,4)
.
We can similarly show that
(
5.23
) is compatible with other relations in (
5.1
)
and (
5.2
).
∎

\bullet

Case 3 with
\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{2}\geq 2
.

Lemma 5.9
.

(Case 3)
Assume
\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{2}\geq 2
.
For
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}

let
\hat{{\varphi}}_{l}
be the functions determined from the functions
\hat{{\varphi}}_{l^{\prime}}

(l^{\prime}=l_{1}e_{1}+l_{4}e_{4}+l_{12}e_{12}+l_{34}e_{34}\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)})

in Lemma
5.5
, and
by the equations (
4.14
), (
4.17
), (
4.18
), (
4.23
),
(
5.1
) and (
5.2
).
Then we have

\displaystyle\hat{{\varphi}}_{l}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3},

where

(5.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})&=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-1-l_{13}-l_{24})_{l_{13}+l_{24}}\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\kappa_{2})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}\\
&\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i)}\,dq.\end{split}

Proof.

Our proof is similar to Lemma
5.8
.
By (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) we know that the formula (
5.24
) is true
for
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},0,0,l_{24},l_{34})
.
By using (
5.1
) and (
5.2
),
we show (
5.24
) by induction on
l_{14}+l_{23}
.
Let
l_{14}+l_{23}\geq 1
.
As in the proof of Lemma
5.8
, if
l_{4}\geq 1
,
(
5.1
) with
i=1
and (
5.2
) with
(i,j,k)=(2,3,4)

tell us

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=\begin{cases}-\widehat{V}_{l-e_{4}-e_{14}+e_{2}+e_{12}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})+\widehat{V}_{l-e_{4}-e_{14}+e_{3}+e_{13}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})&\text{if $l_{14}\geq 1$},\\
-\widehat{V}_{l-e_{4}-e_{23}+e_{2}+e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})+\widehat{V}_{l-e_{4}-e_{23}+e_{3}+e_{24}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})&\text{if $l_{23}\geq 1$}.\end{cases}

The hypothesis of induction implies that the above can be written as

(5.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34}+2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\kappa_{2})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}\\
&\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i)}\,dq\\
&+(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}-1}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-2)_{l_{13}+l_{24}+1}\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\kappa_{2})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}\\
&\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)}\,dq.\end{split}

In view of (
1.3
) and the identity

\displaystyle-(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)

\displaystyle+(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-2)

\displaystyle\times(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)

\displaystyle=(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)

\displaystyle\quad+(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2),

we know that (
5.25
) becomes

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\kappa_{2})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\biggl\{\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}+1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)}\biggr\}\,dq.

We substitute

(q,i)\to(q,i)
and

(q,i)\to(q-1,i-1)
in the first and the second terms in the bracket
\{\cdots\}
, respectively.
Then the formula
\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}{i}+\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-1}{i-1}=\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}}{i}
implies our assertions.
We can similarly show that
(
5.24
) is compatible with other relations in (
5.1
)
and (
5.2
).
∎

\bullet

Case 3 with
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}\geq 2
.

Lemma 5.10
.

(Case 3)
Assume
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}\geq 2
.
For
l=(0,0,0,0,l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2},0)}

let
\hat{{\varphi}}_{l}
be the functions determined from the functions
\hat{{\varphi}}_{l^{\prime}}

(l^{\prime}=l_{12}e_{12}+l_{34}e_{34}\in S_{(\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2},0)})

in Lemma
5.5
, and
by the equations (
4.18
), (
4.23
), (
4.48
), (
4.52
),
(
5.3
) and (
5.4
).
Then we have

\displaystyle\hat{{\varphi}}_{l}(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3},

where

(5.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})&=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\\
&\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\kappa_{2})\\
&\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}\\
&\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i)}\,dq.\end{split}

Proof.

We first confirm (
5.26
) for

l=l_{12}e_{12}+e_{14}+l_{34}e_{34},l_{12}e_{12}+e_{23}+l_{34}e_{34}\in S_{(\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2},0)}
.
From the equations (
4.48
) and (
4.52
) we have

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{l_{12}e_{12}+e_{14}+l_{34}e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=\widehat{V}_{l_{12}e_{12}+e_{23}+l_{34}e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-2}(s_{3}+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-3}{2})(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-3}{2})\widehat{V}_{(l_{12}+1)e_{12}+l_{34}e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2}-1,s_{3}-1)

\displaystyle\quad-\widehat{V}_{(l_{12}+1)e_{12}+l_{34}e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2}-1,s_{3}+1).

In view of the expression (
5.21
) the above can be written as

(5.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\\
&\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\frac{\kappa_{2}-3}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2\kappa_{2})}\\
&\times\{(s_{3}+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-3}{2})(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2\kappa_{2}-2)\\
&\quad-(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-1}{2})(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\}\,dq.\end{split}

Since the bracket
\{\cdots\}
in the above can be written as

\displaystyle(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-3}{2})+(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2\kappa_{2}-2),

we know (
5.27
) becomes

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\times\biggl\{\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\frac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2\kappa_{2})}\,dq

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\frac{\kappa_{2}-3}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\frac{\kappa_{2}+1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}-1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2\kappa_{2}-2)}\,dq\biggr\}

as desired.
Then, by (
5.22
), we know that (
5.26
) holds when
l_{14}+l_{23}=0,1
.

Let us show (
5.26
) by induction on
l_{14}+l_{23}
.
Assume
l_{14}+l_{23}\geq 2
.
From (
5.3
) and (
5.4
), we know

\displaystyle\hat{{\varphi}}_{l}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\hat{{\varphi}}_{l+e_{12}-e_{14}-e_{23}+e_{34}}+\hat{{\varphi}}_{l+e_{13}-e_{14}-e_{23}+e_{24}}&\text{if $l_{14}\geq 1$ and $l_{23}\geq 1$},\\
-\hat{{\varphi}}_{l+2e_{12}-2e_{14}}+\hat{{\varphi}}_{l+2e_{13}-2e_{14}}&\text{if $l_{14}\geq 2$},\\
-\hat{{\varphi}}_{l-2e_{23}+2e_{34}}+\hat{{\varphi}}_{l+2e_{13}-2e_{23}}&\text{if $l_{23}\geq 2$}\end{cases}

for
l=(0,0,0,0,l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{2},\kappa_{2},0)}
.
Then the hypothesis of induction imply that

\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{3\kappa_{2}-1}{2})\cdot(V_{0}+V_{1})

with

\displaystyle V_{p}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{1-p}(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}-2p}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1-2p)_{l_{13}+l_{24}+2p}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+2-2p)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34}+2-2p)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-2p)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i+2-2p)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2p)}\,dq

for
p=0,1
.
In view of the identities

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34}+2)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-2}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})

\displaystyle\times\{(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)

\displaystyle\quad-(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)

\displaystyle\quad-(s_{1}-q+i)(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)

\displaystyle\quad+(s_{1}-q+i)(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\}

and

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}-2}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-3)_{l_{13}+l_{24}+2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{k=0}^{2}\binom{2}{k}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-k)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-2)}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}+k)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-2)},

we find that

\displaystyle V_{0}+V_{1}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})

\displaystyle\quad\times(V_{0;0,0}+V_{0;0,1}+V_{0;1,0}+V_{0;1,1}+V_{1;0}+V_{1;1}+V_{1;2}),

where

\displaystyle V_{0;k_{1},k_{2}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}{i}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+i+2k_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i+2k_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2+2k_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2+2k_{2})}\,dq

for
0\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq 1
, and

\displaystyle V_{1;k}

\displaystyle=\binom{2}{k}\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}-2}{i}\cdot\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-k)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i-2)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}+k)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i-2)}\,dq

for
0\leq k\leq 2
.
Then we know
V_{0;0,0}+V_{1;0}=0
and
V_{0;1,1}+V_{1;2}=0
.
We substitute
i\to i-2
and
(q,i)\to(q-1,i-1)
in
V_{0;1,0}
and
V_{1;1}
, respectively.
Therefore
the formula
\binom{n-2}{i}+2\binom{n-2}{i-1}+\binom{n-2}{i-2}=\binom{n}{i}
leads our assertion.
∎

5.5.
Relations with the Jacquet integrals

We discuss here the relation between the Jacquet integral and
the moderate growth solution
\hat{\varphi}_{l}
in the previous subsections.
As a result, we can remove the assumption (
4.46
) from our main theorems.

First, we recall the definition of the Jacquet integral and its properties.
Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}=\chi_{(\hat{\nu}_{1},\hat{\delta}_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\hat{\nu}_{2},\hat{\delta}_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\hat{\nu}_{3},\hat{\delta}_{3})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\hat{\nu}_{4},\hat{\delta}_{4})}

\displaystyle\text{with}\quad\hat{\nu}=(\hat{\nu}_{1},\hat{\nu}_{2},\hat{\nu}_{3},\hat{\nu}_{4})\in\mathbb{C}^{4},\quad\hat{\delta}=(\hat{\delta}_{1},\hat{\delta}_{2},\hat{\delta}_{3},\hat{\delta}_{4})\in\{0,1\}^{4},

and consider the principal series representation
(\Pi_{\widehat{\sigma}},H(\widehat{\sigma}))
of
G
.
Then we may regard
H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}
as the space of
K
-finite smooth functions
f
on
K
satisfying

\displaystyle f(mk)=m_{1}^{\hat{\delta}_{1}}m_{2}^{\hat{\delta}_{2}}m_{3}^{\hat{\delta}_{3}}m_{4}^{\hat{\delta}_{4}}f(k)

\displaystyle(m=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\nolimits(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4})\in{K}\cap M_{(1,1,1,1)},\ k\in{K}),

and we note that the space
H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}
does not depend on
\hat{\nu}
.
If
\hat{\nu}
satisfies
\mathrm{Re}(\hat{\nu}_{1})>\mathrm{Re}(\hat{\nu}_{2})>\mathrm{Re}(\hat{\nu}_{3})>\mathrm{Re}(\hat{\nu}_{4})
,
for
f\in H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}
,
we define the Jacquet integral
\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}(f)
by the convergent integral

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}(f)(g):=\int_{N}f_{\hat{\nu}}(wxg)\psi_{1}(x)^{-1}dx\quad(g\in G)

with

\displaystyle w=\begin{pmatrix}&&&1\\
&&1&\\
&1&&\\
1&&&\end{pmatrix},

where
f_{\hat{\nu}}
is a smooth function on
G
defined by

\displaystyle f_{\hat{\nu}}(xyk)=y_{1}^{\hat{\nu}_{1}+\frac{3}{2}}y_{2}^{\hat{\nu}_{1}+\hat{\nu}_{2}+2}y_{3}^{\hat{\nu}_{1}+\hat{\nu}_{2}+\hat{\nu}_{3}+\frac{3}{2}}y_{4}^{\hat{\nu}_{1}+\hat{\nu}_{2}+\hat{\nu}_{3}+\hat{\nu}_{4}}f(k)

\displaystyle(x\in N,\ y=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\nolimits(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{3}y_{4},y_{4})\in A,\ k\in K)

with the Iwasawa decomposition
G=NAK
.
By
[
27
, Theorem 15.4.1]
, we know that

\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}(f)(g)
has the holomorphic continuation
to whole
\hat{\nu}\in\mathbb{C}^{4}
for every
f\in H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}
and
g\in G
.
Furthermore, this extends
\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}

to all
\hat{\nu}\in\mathbb{C}^{4}
as a nonzero
G
-homomorphism in

{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{\widehat{\sigma}},\psi_{1}}^{\mathrm{mg}}
.

In this subsection, we do not always assume that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
For each cases 1, 2 and 3 introduced in §
4.1
,
we define the symbols
{p_{0}}
,
\nu
and specify the parameters
\hat{\nu}
,
\hat{\delta}
of
\widehat{\sigma}
as follows:

•

Case 1:
{p_{0}}=4
,
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4})
,
\hat{\nu}=\nu
, and
\hat{\delta}=(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})
.

•

Case 2:
{p_{0}}=3
,
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})
,
\hat{\nu}=(\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{1}-\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})
,
and
\hat{\delta}=(\delta_{1},0,\delta_{2},\delta_{3})
.

•

Case 3:
{p_{0}}=2
,
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
,

\hat{\nu}=(\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{1}-\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2},\nu_{2}-\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})
,
and
\hat{\delta}=(\delta_{1},0,\delta_{2},0)
.

Then we can define
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1}}^{\mathrm{mg}}
by

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}:=\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}\circ\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}
,
where
\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}\colon H(\sigma)\to H(\widehat{\sigma})
is the embedding defined by

\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{H(\sigma)}
, (
3.7
) and (
3.11
)
respectively for cases 1, 2 and 3.
Let
\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}\colon V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}\to H(\sigma)_{K}
be the
K
-embedding
defined in §
3.2
, §
3.3
and §
3.4

respectively for cases 1, 2 and 3.
Here we note that
H(\sigma)_{K}
and
\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}
do not depend on
\nu
.
By the properties of the Jacquet integral
\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 5.11
.

Retain the notation.

(i) The function
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(f)(g)
of
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
is entire
for every
f\in H(\sigma)_{K}
and
g\in G
.
In particular,
the function
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}(\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}(v))(g)
of
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
is entire
for every
v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
and
g\in G
.

(ii) We have
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\neq 0
. In particular, we have
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}\neq 0
if
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.

(iii) We have
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1}}^{\mathrm{mg}}=\mathbb{C}\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}
. In particular, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},{\mathrm{Wh}}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\mathrm{mg}})=\mathbb{C}\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

The statement (i) follows immediately from the entireness of the function

\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}(f)(g)
of
\hat{\nu}\in\mathbb{C}^{4}
for every
f\in H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}
and
g\in G
.
Since the quotient
H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}/\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}(H(\sigma)_{K})
is not large in the sense of Vogan
[
25
]
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbb{C}},K)}\bigl(H(\widehat{\sigma})_{K}/\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}(H(\sigma)_{K}),C^{\infty}(N\backslash G;\psi_{1})_{K}\bigr)=\{0\}

by the result of Matumoto
[
19
, Corollary 2.2.2, Theorem 6.2.1]
.
Hence,
\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}\neq 0
implies
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}=\mathcal{J}_{\widehat{\sigma}}\circ\mathrm{I}_{\sigma}\neq 0
,
and we obtain the statement (ii). By
0\neq\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1}}^{\mathrm{mg}}
and (
1.2
),
we obtain the statement (iii).
∎

We define an open dense subset
\Omega_{0}
of
\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
by

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}:=\begin{cases}\{s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})\in\mathbb{C}^{4}\mid s_{i}-s_{j}\not\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\ \ (1\leq i<j\leq 4),\quad s_{1}+s_{2}\neq s_{3}+s_{4}\}&\text{case 1},\\
\{s=(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\mid s_{i}-s_{j}\not\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\ \ (1\leq i<j\leq 3)\}&\text{case 2},\\
\{s=(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\mid s_{1}-s_{2}\not\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\}&\text{case 3}.\end{cases}

By defintion, we note that (
4.46
) holds if
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
.
Furthermore, by the result of Speh
[
23
, §2]
(see
[
24
]
for more general result),
we know that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible if
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
.
Therefore, if
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, our system of partial differential equations characterize
Whittaker functions for
(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})
at the minimal
K
-type.

Based on the Iwasawa decomposition
G=NAK
, for general
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
,
we define a
K
-homomorphism
\varphi_{\sigma}\colon V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}\to C^{\infty}(N\backslash G;\psi_{1})
by
the equalities

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(v)(xyk)=\psi_{1}(x)\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)(y)

\displaystyle(v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},\ x\in{N},\ y\in{A},\ k\in{K})

and

(5.28)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)=&(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}-l_{13}+l_{24}}(-1)^{l_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}}y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2-\kappa_{2}}y_{4}^{\gamma_{1}}\\
&\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}\end{aligned}

for
y=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\nolimits(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{3}y_{4},y_{4})\in A
and

l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
.
Here
\widehat{V}_{l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
is the Mellin-Barnes kernel of
\hat{\varphi}_{l}
determined in the previous subsections.
Then, if
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, the arguments in the previous subsections imply that
\varphi_{\sigma}
is a unique element of

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\mathrm{mg}})
up to scalar multiple.

For the well-definedness of
\varphi_{\sigma}
for general
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
,
we need to confirm that the definition
(
5.28
) is compatible with the relations of
u_{l}
(
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
) in
Lemma
2.3
, that is, the functions
\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)
(
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
) of
y\in A
defined
by (
5.28
) satisfy the following relations:

•

When
\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}\geq 2
,
for
y\in A
and
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1}-2,\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+2e_{1}})(y)+\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+2e_{2}})(y)+\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+2e_{3}})(y)+\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+2e_{4}})(y)=0.

•

When
\kappa_{1}>\kappa_{2}>0
, for
y\in A
and
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1}-2,\kappa_{2}-1,\delta_{3})}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 4,\,j\neq i}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\nolimits(j-i)\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{j}+e_{ij}})(y)=0

\displaystyle(1\leq i\leq 4),

and

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{i}+e_{jk}})(y)-\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{j}+e_{ik}})(y)+\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{k}+e_{ij}})(y)=0

\displaystyle(1\leq i<j<k\leq 4).

•

When
\kappa_{2}\geq 2
,
for
y\in A
and
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1}-2,\kappa_{2}-2,\delta_{3})}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k\leq 4,\,k\not\in\{i,j\}}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\nolimits((k-i)(k-j))\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{ik}+e_{jk}})(y)=0

\displaystyle(1\leq i,j\leq 4),

and

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{12}+e_{34}})(y)-\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{13}+e_{24}})(y)+\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l+e_{14}+e_{23}})(y)=0.

By the definition (
5.28
), we note that
\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)
is an entire function of
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}

for every
l\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
and
y\in A
.
Since the above relations hold for
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, they also hold for all
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
by the analytic continuation.
Hence,
\varphi_{\sigma}
is well-defined for all
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Retain the notation.

(i) The function
\varphi_{\sigma}(v)(g)
of
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
is entire
for every
v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
and
g\in G
.

(ii) We have
\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})|_{A}\neq 0
.

(iii) If
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},{\mathrm{Wh}}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\mathrm{mg}})=\mathbb{C}\varphi_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

The statement (i) follows from the definition of
\varphi_{\sigma}
.
By the Mellin inversion formula (see
[
8
, Lemma 8.4]
), Lemma
5.4
and
Barnes’ second lemma (Lemma
1.2
),
for
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
with the sufficiently large real parts, we have

(5.29)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})(\hat{y})\,y_{1}^{s_{1}-\frac{3}{2}}y_{2}^{s_{2}-2}y_{3}^{s_{1}+s_{2}+\kappa_{2}-\frac{3}{2}}\frac{dy_{1}}{y_{1}}\frac{dy_{2}}{y_{2}}\frac{dy_{3}}{y_{3}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2})=U_{0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2};r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})

\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+r_{i})\bigr)\bigl(\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+r_{i}+r_{j})\bigr)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4})},

where
\hat{y}={\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3},y_{2}y_{3},y_{3},1)\in A
and

\displaystyle(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=\begin{cases}(\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1},\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4})&\text{cases 1-(i), (ii), (iii)},\\
(\nu_{4}+1,\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{1})&\text{case 1-(iv)},\\
(\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+1}{2},\nu_{2}+\kappa_{2},\nu_{3})&\text{case 2},\\
(\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2},\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+1}{2},\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2},\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}+1}{2})&\text{case 3}.\end{cases}

Hence, the integrand of the left hand side of (
5.29
) is not the zero function,
and we obtain the statement (ii).
The statement (iii) has already been proved above.
∎

By Lemma
5.12
(ii), (iii),
if
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, there is a unique
C(\sigma)\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}=C(\sigma)\varphi_{\sigma}
.
The following proposition allows us to remove the assumption (
4.46
)
from our main theorems.

Proposition 5.13
.

Retain the notation.
Then
C(\sigma)
is extended to whole
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
as an entire function of
\nu
,
and the equality
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma}=C(\sigma)\varphi_{\sigma}
holds for all
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
.
Furthermore, if
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible, we have
C(\sigma)\neq 0
and

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},{\mathrm{Wh}}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\mathrm{mg}})=\mathbb{C}\varphi_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

As a notation in this proof only, for
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
, the symbol
\sigma_{\nu}
denotes
\sigma
corresponding to
\nu
.
Take
s\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
arbitrarily.
By Lemma
5.12
(ii),
we can choose
y_{s}\in A
so that
\varphi_{\sigma_{s}}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})(y_{s})\neq 0
.
Since
\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})(y_{s})
is a continuous function of
\nu
,
we can also choose a open neighborhood
\Omega(s,y_{s})
of
s
so that

\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})(y_{s})\neq 0
(
\nu\in\Omega(s,y_{s})
).
For
\nu\in\Omega(s,y_{s})
, we set

\displaystyle C_{s}(\sigma_{\nu}):=\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{\nu}}(\hat{\eta}_{\sigma_{\nu}}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}}))(y_{s})}{\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}(u_{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{34}})(y_{s})}.

By Lemmas
5.11
(i) and
5.12
(i),
we note that
C_{s}(\sigma_{\nu})
is holomorphic on
\Omega(s,y_{s})
as a function of
\nu
.
Since
\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{\nu}}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma_{\nu}}=C(\sigma_{\nu})\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}
for
\nu\in\Omega_{0}
, we have

(5.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{\nu}}(\hat{\eta}_{\sigma_{\nu}}(v))(g)=C(\sigma_{\nu})\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}(v)(g)

\displaystyle(v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},\ g\in G,\ \nu\in\Omega_{0})

and

(5.31)

\displaystyle C_{s}(\sigma_{\nu})=C(\sigma_{\nu})

\displaystyle(\nu\in\Omega_{0}\cap\Omega(s,y_{s})).

Since we can take
s\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
arbitrarily and
\Omega_{0}
is an open dense subset of
\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
,
the equality (
5.31
) extends
C(\sigma_{\nu})
to whole
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
as an entire function of
\nu
.
By the analytic continuation of the both sides of (
5.30
), we know that

\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{\nu}}\circ\hat{\eta}_{\sigma_{\nu}}=C(\sigma_{\nu})\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}
holds for all
\nu\in\mathbb{C}^{p_{0}}
.
Furthermore, if
\Pi_{\sigma_{\nu}}
is irreducible, Lemma
5.11
(ii), (iii) imply that
C(\sigma_{\nu})\neq 0
and

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},{\mathrm{Wh}}(\Pi_{\sigma_{\nu}},\psi_{1})^{\mathrm{mg}})=\mathbb{C}\varphi_{\sigma_{\nu}}
.
∎

5.6.
Explicit formulas of the minimal
K
-type Whittaker functions

Thanks to the argument of previous subsections, we arrive at explicit formulas of Whittaker functions which is a main result of this paper.
As in Theorem
5.1
, let

y={\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{3}y_{4},y_{4})\in A.

See §
1.4
for the paths of integrations.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
\sigma=\chi_{(\nu_{1},\delta_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{4},\delta_{4})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4}\in\{0,1\}

and
\delta_{1}\geq\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}\geq\delta_{4}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.

(i) When
\delta_{1}=\delta_{2}=\delta_{3}=\delta_{4}
, there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(0,0,\delta_{1})}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{\bf 0})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,\bf 0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,\bf 0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})}\,dq.

(ii)
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,0,0,0)
,
there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(1,0,0)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}}(-1)^{l_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},0,0,0,0,0,0)\in S_{(1,0,0)}
. Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{1}+l_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4})}\,dq.

(iii)
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,0,0)
,
there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(1,1,0)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{13}+l_{24}}(-1)^{l_{14}+l_{23}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(0,0,0,0,l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(1,1,0)}
.
Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,(0,0,0,0,l_{12},l_{13},0,l_{23},0,0)}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+l_{23})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{23})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{13}+l_{23})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{12}+1)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+1)

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{12}+l_{13})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+l_{12})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{12}+l_{23})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{2}+l_{12})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{12}+1)}\,dq,

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,(0,0,0,0,0,0,l_{14},0,l_{24},l_{34})}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{3}+l_{14})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{4}+l_{14})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{14}+l_{24})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{34}+1)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{4}+1)

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{24}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{3}+l_{34})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{14}+l_{34})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{4}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{34}+1)}\,dq.

(iv)
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,1,0)
, there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(1,0,1)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}}(-1)^{l_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},0,0,0,0,0,0)\in S_{(1,0,1)}
. Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{4}+l_{2}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2}+l_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{4}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{4}+l_{3}+l_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{2}+\nu_{4}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+\nu_{2}+\nu_{4}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4})}\,dq.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\kappa_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
,

\delta_{2},\delta_{3}\in\{0,1\}
and

\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.

(i)
When
\delta_{2}=\delta_{3}
,
there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}}(-1)^{l_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},0,0,0,0,0,0)\in S_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}
. Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4})}\,dq.

(ii)
When
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0)
,
there exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}-l_{13}+l_{24}}(-1)^{l_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}
.
Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})_{l_{13}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{24})_{l_{24}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{i_{14}=0}^{l_{14}}\sum_{i_{23}=0}^{l_{23}}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i_{14}+i_{23})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i_{14}+i_{23})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i_{14}-i_{23})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i_{14}-i_{23})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2}+l_{23}+l_{24}+l_{34}+i_{14}-i_{23})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3}+l_{12}+l_{13}+l_{14}-i_{14}+i_{23})\,dq.

Theorem 5.16
.

Let
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes D_{(\nu_{2},\kappa_{2})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and
\kappa_{1}\geq\kappa_{2}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
There exists a
K
-homomorphism

{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

whose radial part is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-l_{1}+l_{3}-l_{13}+l_{24}}(-1)^{l_{2}+l_{14}+l_{23}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}
.
Here

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-l_{13}-l_{24}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24}-1)_{l_{13}+l_{24}}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{12}+l_{34})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{3}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l_{14}+l_{23}}\binom{l_{14}+l_{23}}{i}\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+l_{1}+i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-l_{13}-l_{24})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{1}+l_{3}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{34}+i)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+l_{4}+l_{14}+l_{23}-i)\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+s_{3}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+l_{1}+l_{2}+l_{4}+l_{12}+l_{14}+l_{23}+l_{34}-i)}\,dq.

6.
Test vectors for archimedean Bump-Friedberg integrals

Bump and Friedberg
[
4
]
gave a zeta integral containing two complex variables
which interpolates the standard and the exterior square
L
-functions on
{\rm GL}(n)
simultaneously.
The aim of this section is to give test vectors for this zeta integrals.
The case of principal series is done in
[
11
]
.

6.1.
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors for the standard and the exterior square
L
-functions

We recall the theory of finite dimensional semisimple representations of the Weil group
W_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The Weil group
W_{\mathbb{R}}
for the real field
\mathbb{R}
is given by

W_{\mathbb{R}}={\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\cup(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\,\mathtt{j})\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{\times}
.
Here we regard
W_{\mathbb{R}}
as a subgroup of the multiplicative group
{\mathbb{H}}^{\times}
of the Hamilton quaternion algebra

{\mathbb{H}}=\mathbb{C}\oplus({\mathbb{C}}\,\mathtt{j})
(
\mathtt{j}^{2}=-1
and
\mathtt{j}z\mathtt{j}^{-1}=\overline{z}
for
z\in\mathbb{C}
).
We recall the irreducible representations of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
and the corresponding
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors.

(1)

Characters.

We define characters
\phi^{\delta}_{\nu}\ (\nu\in\mathbb{C},\delta\in\{0,1\})

of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
by

\displaystyle\phi^{\delta}_{\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=|z|^{2\nu}\ \ \ (z\in\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\phi^{\delta}_{\nu}(\mathtt{j})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\delta}.

We define the corresponding
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors by

\displaystyle L(s,\phi^{\delta}_{\nu})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu+\delta),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\phi^{\delta}_{\nu},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\delta}.

(2)

Two dimensional representations.

We define two dimensional representations

\phi_{\nu,\kappa}\colon W_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\rm GL}(2,\mathbb{C})\ (\nu\in\mathbb{C},\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
by

\displaystyle\phi_{\nu,\kappa}(re^{\sqrt{-1}\theta})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}r^{2\nu}e^{-\sqrt{-1}\kappa\theta}&0\\
0&r^{2\nu}e^{\sqrt{-1}\kappa\theta}\end{array}\right)\ \ \ (r\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ \theta\in\mathbb{R}),

\displaystyle\phi_{\nu,\kappa}(\mathtt{j})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&(-1)^{\kappa}\\
1&0\end{array}\right).

The representation
\phi_{\nu,\kappa}
is
irreducible for
\kappa>0
, while
\phi_{\nu,0}
is equivalent to
a sum of two characters
\phi_{\nu}^{0}
and
\phi_{\nu}^{1}
.
For
\phi_{\nu,\kappa}\ (\kappa>0)
, we define the corresponding

L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors by

\displaystyle L(s,\phi_{\nu,\kappa})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu+\tfrac{\kappa}{2}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\phi_{\nu,\kappa},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa+1}.

The set of equivalence classes of
irreducible representations of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
is
exhausted by

\Sigma_{\mathbb{R}}=\left\{\phi_{\nu}^{\delta}\mid\nu\in\mathbb{C},\ \delta\in\{0,1\}\right\}\cup\left\{\phi_{\nu,\kappa}\mid\nu\in\mathbb{C},\ \kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\right\}.

For a finite dimensional semisimple representation
\phi
of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
,
we define the corresponding
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors by

\displaystyle L(s,\phi)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{m}L(s,\phi_{i}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\phi,\psi_{\mathbb{R}})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon(s,\phi_{i},\psi_{\mathbb{R}}),

where

\phi\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\phi_{i}
is the irreducible decomposition of
\phi
.

The local Langlands correspondence on
{\rm GL}(n,\mathbb{R})
is
a bijection between the set of infinitesimal
equivalence classes of irreducible admissible representations
of
{\rm GL}(n,\mathbb{R})
and the set of equivalence classes of
n
-dimensional
semisimple representations of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
.
For an irreducible admissible representation
\Pi
of
{\rm GL}(n,\mathbb{R})
,
the corresponding representation
\phi[\Pi]
of
W_{\mathbb{R}}
is called Langlands parameter of
\Pi.

See
[
17
]
(cf.
[
8
, section 9.1]
) for the precise.
We define the local
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors

L(s,\Pi)
and
\varepsilon(s,\Pi,\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

for the standard
L
-function by

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi)

\displaystyle=L(s,\phi[\Pi]),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi,\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(s,\phi[\Pi],\psi_{\mathbb{R}}).

Let
\wedge^{2}:{\rm GL}(n,\mathbb{C})\to{\rm GL}(\tfrac{n(n-1)}{2},\mathbb{C})
be the exterior square representation.
We define the local
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors

L(s,\Pi,\wedge^{2})
and
\varepsilon(s,\Pi,\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})
for the exterior square
L
-function by

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi,\wedge^{2})

\displaystyle=L(s,\wedge^{2}(\phi[\Pi])),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi,\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(s,\wedge^{2}(\phi[\Pi]),\psi_{\mathbb{R}}).

Let us describe
L
- and
\varepsilon
-factors for the irreducible
P_{\mathbf{n}}
-principal series representation
\Pi_{\sigma}
.
The Langlands parameter
\phi[\Pi_{\sigma}]
is given by

\displaystyle\phi[\Pi_{\sigma}]\cong\begin{cases}\oplus_{1\leq i\leq 4}\phi_{\nu_{i}}^{\delta_{i}}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(1,1,1,1)$},\\
\phi_{\nu_{1},\kappa_{1}-1}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{2}}^{\delta_{2}}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{3}}^{\delta_{3}}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,1,1)$},\\
\phi_{\nu_{1},\kappa_{1}-1}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{2},\kappa_{2}-1}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,2)$}.\end{cases}

We know

\displaystyle\wedge^{2}(\phi[\Pi_{\sigma}])\cong\begin{cases}\oplus_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\phi_{\nu_{i}+\nu_{j}}^{|\delta_{i}-\delta_{j}|}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(1,1,1,1)$},\\
\phi_{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2},\kappa_{1}-1}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{1}+\nu_{3},\kappa_{1}-1}\oplus\phi_{2\nu_{1}}^{\delta_{1}}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}}^{|\delta_{2}-\delta_{3}|}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,1,1)$},\\
\phi_{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2},|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}|}\oplus\phi_{\nu_{1}+\nu_{2},\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}-2}\oplus\phi_{2\nu_{1}}^{\delta_{1}}\oplus\phi_{2\nu_{2}}^{\delta_{2}}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,2)$}.\end{cases}

Here the integers
\delta_{1}\in\{0,1\}
(
\mathbf{n}=(2,1,1)
) and

\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in\{0,1\}
(
\mathbf{n}=(2,2)
) are defined by

\delta_{i}\equiv\kappa_{i}\pmod{2}

(i=1,2)
as in §
4.1
.
Then we have the following.

•

When
\sigma=\chi_{(\nu_{1},\delta_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{4},\delta_{4})}
, we have

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\prod_{1\leq i\leq 4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu_{i}+\delta_{i}),

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu_{i}+\nu_{j}+|\delta_{i}-\delta_{j}|),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\delta_{i}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}|\delta_{i}-\delta_{j}|}.

•

When
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}
, we have

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu_{2}+\delta_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu_{3}+\delta_{3}),

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+2\nu_{1}+\delta_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+|\delta_{2}-\delta_{3}|),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\delta_{2}+\delta_{3}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{2\kappa_{1}+\delta_{1}+|\delta_{2}-\delta_{3}|}.

•

When
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes D_{(\nu_{2},\kappa_{2})}
, we have

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}),

\displaystyle L(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{|\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}|}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}-2}{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+2\nu_{1}+\delta_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+2\nu_{2}+\delta_{2}),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{2\kappa_{1}+\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}}.

6.2.
Archimedean Bump-Friedberg integrals

We recall the archimedean part of Bump-Friedberg integrals.
As in §
1.1
,
we set
G_{2}={\rm GL}(2,\mathbb{R})
,

N_{2}=\{(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x_{1,2}\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix})\mid x_{1,2}\in\mathbb{R}\}
,
K_{2}={\rm O}(2)

and
\mathrm{k}_{\theta}^{(2)}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\\
-\sin\theta&\cos\theta\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in K_{2}.

We define an embedding
\tilde{\iota}:G_{2}\times G_{2}\to G
by

\displaystyle\left(g_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
c_{1}&d_{1}\end{pmatrix},g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
c_{2}&d_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right)\mapsto\tilde{\iota}(g_{1},g_{2}):=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&&b_{1}&\\
&a_{2}&&b_{2}\\
c_{1}&&d_{1}&\\
&c_{2}&&d_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be the space of Schwartz-Bruhat functions on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
For
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\Phi\in{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
W\in{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}
,
we consider the following archimedean zeta integral:

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{N_{2}\backslash G_{2}}\int_{N_{2}\backslash G_{2}}W(\tilde{\iota}(g_{1},g_{2}))\Phi((0,1)g_{2})|\det g_{1}|^{s_{1}-\frac{1}{2}}|\det g_{2}|^{-s_{1}+s_{2}+\frac{1}{2}}\,d\dot{g}_{1}d\dot{g}_{2}.

Here

d\dot{g}
is the right
G_{2}
-invariant measure on

N_{2}\backslash G_{2}
normalized so that

\displaystyle\int_{N_{2}\backslash G_{2}}f(g)\,d\dot{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{K_{2}}f\bigl({\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2},y_{2})k\bigr)\,dk\dfrac{2dy_{1}}{y_{1}^{2}}\dfrac{2dy_{2}}{y_{2}}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}f\bigl({\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2},y_{2})\,{\rm diag}(\varepsilon,1)\,\mathrm{k}_{\theta}^{(2)}\bigr)\,\dfrac{d\theta}{2\pi}\dfrac{dy_{1}}{y_{1}^{2}}\dfrac{dy_{2}}{y_{2}}

for any compactly supported continuous function
f
on
N_{2}\backslash G_{2}.

Here
dk
is the normalized Haar measure on
K_{2}
such that
\int_{K_{2}}dk=1
.
For
\Phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
we define the Fourier transform
\widehat{\Phi}
of
\Phi
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi(y_{1},y_{2})\psi_{\mathbb{R}}(x_{1}y_{1}+x_{2}y_{2})\,dy_{1}dy_{2}.

For
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
we put

\displaystyle\Phi_{(a,b)}(x_{1},x_{2})=(-\sqrt{-1}x_{1}+x_{2})^{a}(\sqrt{-1}x_{1}+x_{2})^{b}\exp\{-\pi(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})\}.

We write
g_{i}\in N_{2}\backslash G_{2}

(i=1,2)
as

g_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}y_{i1}y_{i2}&\\
&y_{i2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon_{i}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{i}}^{(2)}

with
y_{i1},y_{i2}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\varepsilon_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
and

0\leq\theta_{i}<2\pi
,
to find that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=2^{2}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl({\rm diag}(y_{11}y_{12},y_{21}y_{22},y_{12},y_{22})\,{\rm diag}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},1,1)\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\Phi_{(a,b)}(-y_{22}\sin\theta_{2},y_{22}\cos\theta_{2})\,(y_{11}y_{12}^{2})^{s_{1}-\frac{1}{2}}(y_{21}y_{22}^{2})^{-s_{1}+s_{2}+\frac{1}{2}}\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dy_{11}}{y_{11}^{2}}\frac{dy_{12}}{y_{12}}\frac{dy_{21}}{y_{21}^{2}}\frac{dy_{22}}{y_{22}}.

Now we change the variables
(y_{11},y_{12},y_{21},y_{22})\to(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})
by

\displaystyle y_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{11}y_{12}}{y_{21}y_{22}},

\displaystyle y_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{21}y_{22}}{y_{12}},

\displaystyle y_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{12}}{y_{22}},

\displaystyle y_{4}

\displaystyle=y_{22}

and write

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{3}y_{4},y_{4})\in A,

\displaystyle\hat{y}

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3},y_{2}y_{3},y_{3},1)\in A.

Then we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=2^{2}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl({\rm diag}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},1,1)\,y\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\times y_{1}^{s_{1}-\frac{3}{2}}y_{2}^{s_{2}-2}y_{3}^{s_{1}+s_{2}-\frac{3}{2}}y_{4}^{2s_{2}+a+b}\exp(-\pi y_{4}^{2})\exp\{\sqrt{-1}(a-b)\theta_{2}\}\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dy_{1}}{y_{1}}\frac{dy_{2}}{y_{2}}\frac{dy_{3}}{y_{3}}\frac{dy_{4}}{y_{4}}.

Since

\displaystyle W\bigl({\rm diag}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},1,1)\,y\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)=y_{4}^{\gamma_{1}}W\bigl({\rm diag}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},1,1)\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr),

the formula

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-\pi x^{2})\,x^{s}\frac{dx}{x}=2^{-1}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s)

implies that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=2\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a+b)\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl(m_{i}\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\exp\{\sqrt{-1}(a-b)\theta_{2}\}\,y_{1}^{s_{1}-\frac{3}{2}}y_{2}^{s_{2}-2}y_{3}^{s_{1}+s_{2}-\frac{3}{2}}\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}\frac{dy_{1}}{y_{1}}\frac{dy_{2}}{y_{2}}\frac{dy_{3}}{y_{3}}.

Here we defined
m_{i}

(0\leq i\leq 3)
by

\displaystyle m_{0}

\displaystyle=1_{4},

\displaystyle m_{1}

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(-1,1,1,1),

\displaystyle m_{2}

\displaystyle={\rm diag}(1,-1,1,1),

\displaystyle m_{3}

\displaystyle=m_{1}m_{2}.

Lemma 6.1
.

Retain the notation.
Let

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})=2^{-2}\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl(m_{i}\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)\exp\{\sqrt{-1}(a-b)\theta_{2}\}\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}.

Assume that
W^{\prime}
admits the Mellin-Barnes integral representaiton

W^{\prime}(\hat{y})=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}\cdot\dfrac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3},

where the path of integration

\int_{s_{i}}
is the vertical line from

{\rm Re}(s_{i})-\sqrt{-1}\infty
to
{\rm Re}(s_{i})+\sqrt{-1}\infty
with sufficiently large real part
to keep the poles of
V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
on its left.
Then we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(a.b)})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a+b)V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2}).

Proof.

It is immediate from Mellin inversion
(see
[
8
, Lemma 8.4]
).
∎

We use the following formula to evaluate archimedean zeta integrals.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
a,b,c\in\mathbb{C}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

such that
{\rm Re}(a),{\rm Re}(b)>0
and
{\rm Re}(c)>2m
, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{0\leq j\leq m}\binom{m}{j}\dfrac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+2j)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b+2j)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(c-2j)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+b+c+2j-2m)}=\dfrac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+c)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b+c)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(c-2m)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+b+c)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+c-2m)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b+c-2m)}.\end{split}

Proof.

Using the equalities

(6.1)

\displaystyle(z)_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2z+2j)}{\pi^{-j}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2z)}=(-1)^{j}\frac{\pi^{j}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2-2z)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2-2z-2j)}

\displaystyle(z\in\mathbb{C},\,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}),

and

\binom{m}{j}=(-1)^{j}(-m)_{j}/j!
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{0\leq j\leq m}\binom{m}{j}\dfrac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+2j)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b+2j)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(c-2j)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+b+c+2j-2m)}=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(b)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(c)}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(a+b+c-2m)}\,{}_{3}F_{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-m,\ \frac{a}{2},\ \frac{b}{2}\\
1-\frac{c}{2},\ \frac{a+b+c}{2}-m\end{array};1\right)\end{split}

with the generalized hypergeometric series

\displaystyle{}_{3}F_{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}a_{1},\ a_{2},\ a_{3}\\
b_{1},\ b_{2}\end{array};z\right)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(a_{1})_{j}(a_{2})_{j}(a_{3})_{j}}{(b_{1})_{j}(b_{2})_{j}}\frac{z^{j}}{j!}.

Applying the Saalschütz’s theorem (
[
1
, 2.2 (1)]
)

\displaystyle{}_{3}F_{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-m,\ a,\ b\\
c,\ 1+a+b-c-m\end{array};1\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(c-a)_{m}(c-b)_{m}}{(c)_{m}(c-a-b)_{m}}

\displaystyle(m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})

and (
6.1
), we obtain the assertion.
∎

6.3.
Contragredient Whittaker functions

Let
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma}
be the contragredient representation of
\Pi_{\sigma}
.
We have

\displaystyle{\rm Wh}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma},\psi_{-1})^{\rm mg}=\{\widetilde{W}\mid W\in{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}\},

where the contragredient Whittaker function
\widetilde{W}
is defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(g)

\displaystyle:=W(w\,{}^{t}\!g^{-1}),

\displaystyle w=\begin{pmatrix}&&&1\\
&&1&\\
&1&&\\
1&&&\end{pmatrix}.

Let
\tilde{\sigma}
be a representation of
M_{\mathbf{n}}
defined by replacing
\nu_{i}
to
-\nu_{i}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\chi_{(-\nu_{1},\delta_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(-\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(-\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}\boxtimes\chi_{(-\nu_{4},\delta_{4})}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(1,1,1,1)$},\\
D_{(-\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(-\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(-\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,1,1)$},\\
D_{(-\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes D_{(-\nu_{2},\kappa_{2})}&\text{if $\mathbf{n}=(2,2)$}.\end{cases}

Then we know
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma}\cong\Pi_{\tilde{\sigma}}

and the following:

Proposition 6.3
.

For the
K
-homomorphisms
{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

given in Theorems
5.14
,
5.15
and
5.16
,
let
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}\to{\rm Wh}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma},\psi_{-1})^{\rm mg}

be a
K
-homomorphism defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(v)(g)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\sigma}(v)(w\,{}^{t}\!g^{-1})

\displaystyle(v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}).

Then the radial part of
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=y_{1}^{3/2}y_{2}^{2}y_{3}^{3/2}y_{4}^{-\gamma_{1}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{l_{2}-l_{4}+l_{13}-l_{24}}(-1)^{\kappa_{2}+l_{3}+l_{14}+l_{23}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}}\int_{s_{3}}\int_{s_{2}}\int_{s_{1}}V_{\tilde{\sigma},l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,y_{1}^{-s_{1}}y_{2}^{-s_{2}}y_{3}^{-s_{3}}\,ds_{1}ds_{2}ds_{3}

with
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
.

Proof.

For
l=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3},l_{4},l_{12},l_{13},l_{14},l_{23},l_{24},l_{34})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
,
if we set

\displaystyle\tilde{l}=(l_{4},l_{3},l_{2},l_{1},l_{34},l_{24},l_{23},l_{14},l_{13},l_{12})\in S_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})},

then we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{l})(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{{\sigma}}(u_{l})(w\,{}^{t}y^{-1}w^{-1}\cdot w)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(w)u_{l})(w\,{}^{t}y^{-1}w^{-1})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\kappa_{2}}\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{\tilde{l}})\left({\rm diag}(y_{4}^{-1},(y_{3}y_{4})^{-1},(y_{2}y_{3}y_{4})^{-1},(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4})^{-1})\right).

Since the explicit formulas of
V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

in Theorems
5.14
,
5.15
and
5.16
tell us that

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,\tilde{l}}(s_{3}-\gamma_{1},s_{2}-\gamma_{1},s_{1}-\gamma_{1})

\displaystyle=V_{\tilde{\sigma},l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}),

our claim follows.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Retain the notation. Let
v\in V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}
.

(i) If
W=\varphi_{\sigma}(v)
, then we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(gk)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)(g)

for
(g,k)\in G\times K
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(m_{p}y)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(m_{p})v)(y)

for
0\leq p\leq 3
and
y\in A
.

(ii)
If
W=R(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}})\varphi_{\sigma}(v)

(1\leq i<j\leq 4)
, then we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(gk)

\displaystyle=-R({\rm Ad}(k)(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}}))\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)(g)

for
(g,k)\in G\times K
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(m_{p}y)

\displaystyle=-R({\rm Ad}(m_{p})(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}}))\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(m_{p})v)(y),

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(y)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}4\pi\sqrt{-1}y_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(v)(y)+\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)&\text{if $j=i+1$},\\
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)&\text{if $j>i+1$}\end{cases}

for
0\leq p\leq 3
and
y={\rm diag}(y_{1}y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{2}y_{3}y_{4},y_{3}y_{4},y_{4})\in A
.

Proof.

Our statement (i) is obvious.
Let us show (ii).
We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(gk)

\displaystyle=W(w\,{}^{t}\!g^{-1}k)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\varphi_{\sigma}(v)\big(w\,{}^{t}\!g^{-1}k\exp(tE_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}})k^{-1}\cdot k\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)\big(w\,{}^{t}(gk\exp(-tE_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}})k^{-1})^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)\big(w\,{}^{t}(gk\exp(tE_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}})k^{-1})^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)\big(gk\exp(tE_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}})k^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=-R({\rm Ad}(k)(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}}))\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(k)v)(g).

Our claim for
\widetilde{W}(m_{p}y)
readily follows from the above.
As for the radial part, in view of
E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}}=2E_{j,i}+E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}(y)

\displaystyle=2\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\varphi_{\sigma}(v)\bigl(w\,{}^{t}y^{-1}\exp(tE_{j,i})\bigr)+\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)

\displaystyle=2\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\varphi_{\sigma}(v)\bigl(w\,{}^{t}(y\exp(-tE_{i,j}))^{-1}\bigr)+\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)

\displaystyle=-2\frac{d}{dt}\biggl|_{t=0}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(v)(y\exp(tE_{i,j}))+\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)

\displaystyle=-2R(E_{i,j})\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(v)(y)+\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\delta_{3})}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})v)(y)

to get the assertion.
∎

6.4.
Test vectors and main results

To compute
W^{\prime}(\hat{y})
in Lemma
6.1
,
we consider the actions of
E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}}
,
E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}}

and
m_{i}

(i=0,1,2,3)
on
U(\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})\otimes V_{\lambda}
.
For

w=\sum_{i}c_{i}E_{p_{i},q_{i}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{l_{i}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda}

(c_{i}\in\mathbb{C},\,{l_{i}}\in S_{\lambda})

we write

\overline{w}=\sum_{i}\overline{c_{i}}E_{p_{i},q_{i}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{l_{i}}.

Lemma 6.5

(
[
11
, Proposition 2.4]
)
.

For
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4}\in\{0,1\}
with

\delta_{1}\geq\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}\geq\delta_{4}
,
we set
\lambda=(\delta_{1}-\delta_{4},\delta_{2}-\delta_{3},\delta_{3})
.

(a)

When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(0,0,0,0)
, if we set

w=u_{{\bf 0}}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(b)

When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,0,0,0)
, if we set

w=u_{e_{2}}+\sqrt{-1}u_{e_{4}}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}w,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
-\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

(c)

When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,0,0)
, if we set

w=E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{e_{12}}-E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{e_{23}}+E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{e_{34}}+E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes u_{e_{14}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})w

\displaystyle=({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{p}})w=0,

\displaystyle({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(d)

When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,1,0)
, if we set

w=(-E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\sqrt{-1}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes u_{e_{1}}+(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\sqrt{-1}E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes u_{e_{3}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,\quad({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=\sqrt{-1}w,\quad({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
-\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

(e)

When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,1,1)
, if we set

w=(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}-E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes u_{\bf 0}\in U(\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}})\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})w=({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{p}})w=0,\quad({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

Proof.

Use Lemma
2.4
(i) and the formulas

\displaystyle{\rm ad}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})E_{p,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{1,p}E_{3,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\delta_{1,q}E_{3,p}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\delta_{3,p}E_{1,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\delta_{3,q}E_{1,p}^{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle{\rm ad}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})E_{p,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{2,p}E_{4,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\delta_{2,q}E_{4,p}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\delta_{4,p}E_{2,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\delta_{4,q}E_{2,p}^{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle{\rm Ad}(m_{i})E_{p,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\delta_{i,p}+\delta_{i,q}}E_{p,q}^{\mathfrak{p}}\qquad(i=1,2)

for
1\leq p,q\leq 4.

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

For
\kappa_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and

\delta_{2},\delta_{3}\in\{0,1\}
with
\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}
,
we set

\lambda=(\kappa_{1},\delta_{2}-\delta_{3},\delta_{3})
.
For
1\leq p,q,r\leq 4
we define
w_{0},w_{p},w_{p,q},w_{pq},w_{p,qr}\in V_{\lambda}

as follows:

•

When
\kappa_{1}
is even and
\delta_{2}=\delta_{3}
, we put

\displaystyle w_{0}:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{\kappa_{1}/2}\bigr),\quad w_{p,q}:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-2)/2}\xi_{p}\xi_{q}\bigr).

•

When
\kappa_{1}
is even and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0)
, we put

\displaystyle w_{p,qr}:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-2)/2}\xi_{p}\xi_{qr}\bigr).

•

When
\kappa_{1}
is odd and
\delta_{2}=\delta_{3}
, we put

\displaystyle w_{p}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2}\bigr)^{(\kappa_{1}-1)/2}\xi_{p}).

•

When
\kappa_{1}
is odd and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0)
, we put

\displaystyle w_{pq}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-1)/2}\xi_{pq}\bigr).

Then we have the following.

(a)

When
\kappa_{1}
is even and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(0,0)
, if we set

w=w_{0}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(b)

When
\kappa_{1}
is odd and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(0,0)
, if we set

w=w_{2}+\sqrt{-1}w_{4}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}w,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
-\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

(c)

When
\kappa_{1}
is even and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,1)
, if we set

w=E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{3,4}-E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{1,4}+E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{1,2}-E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{2,3}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,

\displaystyle({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(d)

When
\kappa_{1}
is odd and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,1)
, if we set

w=(-E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\sqrt{-1}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})\otimes w_{1}+(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\sqrt{-1}E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}})w_{3}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,\quad({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=\sqrt{-1}w,\quad({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
-\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

(e)

When
\kappa_{1}
is even and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0)
, if we set

w=-\sqrt{-1}w_{2,24}+w_{4,24}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}w,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
-\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

(f)

When
\kappa_{1}
is odd and
(\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0)
, if we set

w=E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{12}-E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{23}+E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{34}+E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{14}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,

\displaystyle({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

Proof.

Since

\mathcal{T}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{n})=\mathcal{T}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{n})=0

(n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
,
we know that

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{0}

\displaystyle=\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{0}=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3,p}w_{1}-\delta_{1,p}w_{3},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p}

\displaystyle=\delta_{4,p}w_{2}-\delta_{2,p}w_{4},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3,p}w_{1,q}-\delta_{1,p}w_{3,q}+\delta_{3,q}w_{p,1}-\delta_{1,q}w_{p,3},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,\delta_{3})}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\delta_{4,p}w_{2,q}-\delta_{2,p}w_{4,q}+\delta_{4,q}w_{p,2}-\delta_{2,q}w_{p,4},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{2,24}

\displaystyle=\tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{4,24}=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{2,24}

\displaystyle=-w_{4,24},\ \ \tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{4,24}=w_{2,24},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{pq}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3,p}w_{1q}-\delta_{1,p}w_{3q}+\delta_{3,q}w_{p1}-\delta_{1,q}w_{p3},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},1,0)}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{pq}

\displaystyle=\delta_{4,p}w_{2q}-\delta_{2,p}w_{4q}+\delta_{4,q}w_{p2}-\delta_{2,q}w_{p4}.

Note that
w_{p,q}=w_{q,p}
and
w_{pq}=-w_{qp}.

Combined with the formulas in the proof of Lemma
6.5
, we can get the assertion.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

For
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
with
\kappa_{1}\geq\kappa_{2}
,
we set
\lambda=(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)
.
For
1\leq p,q\leq 4
we define
w_{0},w_{p},w_{pq}\in V_{\lambda}
as follows:

•

When
\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}
is even, we put

\displaystyle w_{0}:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})/2}\xi_{24}^{\kappa_{2}}\bigr),\quad w_{pq}:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2})/2}\xi_{pq}\xi_{24}^{\kappa_{2}-1}\bigr).

•

When
\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}
is odd, we put

\displaystyle w_{p}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1)/2}\xi_{p}\xi_{24}^{\kappa_{2}}\bigr).

Then we have the following.

(a)

When
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are even, if we set

w=w_{0}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(b)

When
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
are odd, if we set

w=E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{{12}}-E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{{23}}+E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{{34}}+E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes w_{{14}}\in\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=({\rm ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w=0,
