\displaystyle({\rm Ad}\otimes\tau_{\lambda})(m_{i})w=w\quad(0\leq i\leq 3).

(c)

When
\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}
is odd, if we set

w=w_{2}+\sqrt{-1}w_{4}\in V_{\lambda},

then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}w,

\displaystyle\tau_{\lambda}(m_{i})w

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}w&\mbox{ if }i=0,1,\\
(-1)^{\kappa_{1}}\overline{w}&\mbox{ if }i=2,3.\end{cases}

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3,p}w_{1}-\delta_{1,p}w_{3},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{p}

\displaystyle=\delta_{4,p}w_{2}-\delta_{2,p}w_{4},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{pq}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3,p}w_{1q}-\delta_{1,p}w_{3q}+\delta_{3,q}w_{p1}-\delta_{1,q}w_{p3},

\displaystyle\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{2,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{pq}

\displaystyle=\delta_{4,p}w_{2q}-\delta_{2,p}w_{4q}+\delta_{4,q}w_{p2}-\delta_{2,q}w_{p4}.

∎

Now we can state our main results for archimedean zeta integrals.

Theorem 6.8

(
[
11
, Theorem 4.1]
)
.

Let
\sigma=\chi_{(\nu_{1},\delta_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{4},\delta_{4})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4}\in\mathbb{C}
,

\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4}\in\{0,1\}
and

\delta_{1}\geq\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}\geq\delta_{4}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
Let
\varphi_{\sigma}:V_{(\delta_{1}-\delta_{4},\delta_{2}-\delta_{3},\delta_{3})}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

be the
K
-homomorphism given in Theorem
5.14
.
We define
W\in{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}
and
\Phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

as follows.

•

Case 1-(a):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(0,0,0,0)
, we set

W=\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{\bf 0}).

•

Case 1-(b):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,0,0,0)
, we set

W=(-\sqrt{-1})\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{e_{2}}+\sqrt{-1}u_{e_{4}}).

•

Case 1-(c):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,0,0)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{12}})-R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{23}})+R(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{34}})+R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{14}})\}.

•

Case 1-(d):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,1,0)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(-E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\sqrt{-1}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{1}})+R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\sqrt{-1}E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{3}})\}.

•

Case 1-(e):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4})=(1,1,1,1)
, we set

W=(4\pi)^{-2}R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}-E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{\bf 0}).

Here
R
is the right differential.
Further we choose
b\in\{0,1\}
such that

b\equiv\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}+\delta_{3}+\delta_{4}\pmod{2}
,
and set
\Phi=\Phi_{(0,b)}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=L(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma})L(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}),

\displaystyle Z(1-s_{1},1-s_{2},\widetilde{W},\widehat{\Phi})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\varepsilon(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})L(1-s_{1},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma})L(1-s_{2},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}).

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{2},\delta_{2})}\boxtimes\chi_{(\nu_{3},\delta_{3})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\kappa_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
,

\delta_{2},\delta_{3}\in\{0,1\}
and

\delta_{2}\geq\delta_{3}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
We define
\delta_{1}\in\{0,1\}
by
\delta_{1}\equiv\kappa_{1}\pmod{2}
.
Let
\varphi_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},\delta_{2}-\delta_{3},\delta_{3})}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

be the
K
-homomorphism given in Theorem
5.15
.
Under the notation in Lemma
6.6
,
we define
W\in{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}
and
\Phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

as follows.

•

Case 2-(a):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(0,0,0)
, we set

W=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{0}).

•

Case 2-(b):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,0,0)
, we set

W=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{2}+\sqrt{-1}w_{4}).

•

Case 2-(c):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(0,1,1)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{3,4})-R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{1,4})+R(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{1,2})-R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{2,3})\}.

•

Case 2-(d):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,1,1)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(-E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}+\sqrt{-1}E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{1})+R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}-\sqrt{-1}E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{3})\}.

•

Case 2-(e):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(0,1,0)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(-\sqrt{-1}w_{2,24}+w_{4,24}).

•

Case 2-(f):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3})=(1,1,0)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{12})-R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{23})+R(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{34})+R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{14})\}.

Here
R
is the right differential.
Further we choose
b\in\{0,1\}
such that

b\equiv\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}+\delta_{3}\pmod{2}
,
and set
\Phi=\Phi_{(0,b)}
. Then we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=L(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma})L(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}),

\displaystyle Z(1-s_{1},1-s_{2},\widetilde{W},\widehat{\Phi})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\varepsilon(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})L(1-s_{1},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma})L(1-s_{2},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}).

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
\sigma=D_{(\nu_{1},\kappa_{1})}\boxtimes D_{(\nu_{2},\kappa_{2})}

with
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}

and
\kappa_{1}\geq\kappa_{2}

such that
\Pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
We define
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in\{0,1\}
by
\delta_{1}\equiv\kappa_{1}
,
\delta_{2}\equiv\kappa_{2}\pmod{2}
.
Let
\varphi_{\sigma}:V_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}\to{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}

be the
K
-homomorphism given in Theorem
5.16
.
Under the notation in Lemma
6.7
,
we define
W\in{\rm Wh}(\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{1})^{\rm mg}
and
\Phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

as follows.

•

Case 3-(a):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})=(0,0)
, we set

\displaystyle W=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{0}).

•

Case 3-(b):
When
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})=(1,1)
, we set

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{12})-R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{23})+R(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{34})+R(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{p}}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{14})\}.

•

Case 3-(c):
When
\delta_{1}\neq\delta_{2}
, we set

\displaystyle W=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\delta_{2}}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(w_{2}+\sqrt{-1}w_{4}).

Here
R
is the right differential.
Further we choose
b\in\{0,1\}
such that

b\equiv\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}\pmod{2}
,
and set
\Phi=\Phi_{(0,b)}
. Then we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=L(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma})L(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}),

\displaystyle Z(1-s_{1},1-s_{2},\widetilde{W},\widehat{\Phi})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(s_{1},\Pi_{\sigma},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})\varepsilon(s_{2},\Pi_{\sigma},\wedge^{2},\psi_{\mathbb{R}})L(1-s_{1},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma})L(1-s_{2},\widetilde{\Pi}_{\sigma},\wedge^{2}).

In the next three subsections we prove Theorems
6.8
,
6.9
and
6.10
.

6.5.
Proof of Theorem
6.8

By Lemma
6.5
, we know that

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=2^{-2}\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl(m_{i}\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)\exp(-\sqrt{-1}b\,\theta_{2})\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}W(\hat{y})&\mbox{cases 1-(a), (c), (e)},\\
\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{e_{4}})(\hat{y})&\mbox{case 1-(b)},\\
(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{e_{1}})(\hat{y})-R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}})\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{e_{3}})(\hat{y})\}&\mbox{case 1-(d)}.\end{cases}

Then Lemma
6.1
implies that

(6.2)

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(0,b)})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+b)V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2}).

Here we use the notation
\gamma_{1}=\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4}.

We express the Mellin-Barnes kernel
V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
of
W(\hat{y})

in terms of
V_{\sigma,l}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
given in Theorem
5.14
, and compute the zeta integral
by using the following lemma.

Lemma 6.11
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3},\mu_{4})\in\mathbb{C}^{4}
, we set

\displaystyle A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},a_{7};\mu)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+a_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\mu_{1}+a_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\mu_{2}+a_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+a_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+a_{5})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\mu_{1}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+a_{6})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+a_{7}),

\displaystyle B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5},b_{6};b_{7},b_{8};\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+b_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\mu_{1}+b_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}-q+\mu_{2}+b_{3})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+b_{7})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+b_{8})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+b_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\mu_{3}+b_{5})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\mu_{4}+b_{6})

and

\displaystyle{B}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5},b_{6};b_{7},b_{8};\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5},b_{6};b_{7},b_{8};\mu)\,dq.

When
b_{4}=b_{7}
,

B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5},b_{6};b_{7},b_{8};\mu)
and

{B}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5},b_{6};b_{7},b_{8};\mu)
are denoted
by
B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{5},b_{6};b_{8};\mu)
and
B(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{5},b_{6};b_{8};\mu),
respectively.
If
b_{8}=b_{1}+b_{2}+b_{3}+b_{5}+b_{6}
,
a_{6}=b_{1}+b_{2}+b_{5}+b_{6}
and

a_{7}=b_{1}+b_{3}+b_{5}+b_{6}
, then we have

\displaystyle A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},a_{7};\mu){B}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{5},b_{6};b_{8};\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+a_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}+b_{2}+b_{3}+b_{5}+b_{6})}

\displaystyle\quad\times L_{1}(a_{2},a_{3},b_{1}+b_{5},b_{1}+b_{6};\mu)L_{2}(a_{4},b_{2}+b_{5},b_{2}+b_{6},b_{3}+b_{5},b_{3}+b_{6},a_{5};\mu),

where

\displaystyle L_{1}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4};\mu)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i\leq 4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\mu_{i}+\alpha_{i}),

\displaystyle L_{2}(\alpha_{1,2},\alpha_{1,3},\alpha_{1,4},\alpha_{2,3},\alpha_{2,4},\alpha_{3,4};\mu)

\displaystyle=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\mu_{i}+\mu_{j}+\alpha_{i,j}).

Proof.

It is immediate from Lemma
1.2
.
∎

Let us compute the zeta integral
Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi).

We write
\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4})
and
\nu^{\prime}=(\nu_{4},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{1})
.

Case 1-(a):

V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=V_{\sigma,{\bf 0}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
,
(
6.2
), Theorem
5.14
(i) and Lemma
6.11
with
\mu=\nu
imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})V_{\sigma,\bf 0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2})

\displaystyle=A(0,0,0,0,0,0,0;\nu){B}(0,0,0,0,0;0;\nu)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,0,0,0;\nu)L_{2}(0,0,0,0,0,0;\nu).

Case 1-(b):

V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=V_{\sigma,e_{4}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
,
(
6.2
),
Theorem
5.14
(ii) and Lemma
6.11
with
\mu=\nu
imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+1)V_{\sigma,e_{4}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2})

\displaystyle=A(1,1,0,1,0,1,0;\nu)B(0,1,0,0,0;1;\nu)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(1,0,0,0;\nu)L_{2}(1,1,1,0,0,0;\nu).

Case 1-(c):
By using
E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{p}}=2E_{i,j}-E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}}
, we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\{R(2E_{1,2}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{12}})(\hat{y})-{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,1,0)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{12}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad-R(2E_{2,3}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{23}})(\hat{y})+{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,1,0)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{23}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{3,4}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{34}})(\hat{y})-{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,1,0)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{34}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{1,4}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{14}})(\hat{y})-{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,1,0)}(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{14}})(\hat{y})\}.

In view of Lemma
4.1
and
\tau_{(1,1,0)}(E_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{ij}}=0
, we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})=y_{1}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{12}})(\hat{y})-y_{2}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{23}})(\hat{y})+y_{3}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{34}})(\hat{y}),

that is,

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=V_{\sigma,e_{12}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{3})+V_{\sigma,e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{3})+V_{\sigma,e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}+1).

Then (
6.2
) and Theorem
5.14
(iii) imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})\{V_{\sigma,e_{12}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2})

\displaystyle\quad+V_{\sigma,e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{1}+s_{2})+V_{\sigma,e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2}+1)\}

\displaystyle=A(0,1,1,0,2,1,1;\nu){B}(2,1,1,0,0,0;2,2;\nu)

\displaystyle\quad+A(0,1,1,2,2,1,1;\nu){B}(0,1,1,0,0,0;2,2;\nu)

\displaystyle\quad+A(0,1,1,2,0,1,1;\nu){B}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu).

Here we used the expression

V_{\sigma,e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=U_{0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}-1;\nu_{1}+1,\nu_{2}+1,\nu_{3},\nu_{4})
(Lemma
5.3
(ii)).
In view of (
1.3
), we know

\displaystyle A(0,1,1,0,2,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(2,1,1,0,0,0;2,2;\nu)+A(0,1,1,2,2,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0,0;2,2;\nu)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+A(0,1,1,2,0,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu)

\displaystyle={(2\pi)}^{-1}\{(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2})+(s_{1}-q)\}A(0,1,1,0,2,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0,0;2,2;\nu)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+A(0,1,1,2,0,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu)

\displaystyle=\{A(0,1,1,0,2,1,1;\nu)+A(0,1,1,2,0,1,1;\nu)\}B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})\cdot A(0,1,1,0,0,1,1;\nu)B_{0}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu).

Then Lemma
6.11
with
\mu=\nu
leads us that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})A(0,1,1,0,0,1,1;\nu){B}(0,1,1,0,0;2;\nu)

\displaystyle=\frac{2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+2)}\cdot L_{1}(1,1,0,0;\nu)L_{2}(0,1,1,1,1,0;\nu)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(1,1,0,0;\nu)L_{2}(0,1,1,1,1,0;\nu).

Case 1-(d):
As is the case 1-(c), we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(2E_{2,3}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{1}})(\hat{y})-{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,0,1)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{1}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad-R(2E_{1,2}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{3}})(\hat{y})+{\varphi}_{\sigma}(\tau_{(1,0,1)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})u_{e_{3}})(\hat{y})\}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}y_{2}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{1}})(\hat{y})-\sqrt{-1}y_{1}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{e_{3}})(\hat{y}),

that is,

V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=V_{\sigma,e_{1}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{3})+V_{\sigma,e_{3}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{3}).

Then (
6.2
) and Theorem
5.14
(iv) imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=A(1,0,1,1,2,0,1;\nu^{\prime}){B}(1,1,2,0,0,0;2,2;\nu^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+A(1,2,1,1,0,0,1;\nu^{\prime}){B}(1,1,0,0,0,0;2,0;\nu^{\prime}).

In view of

\displaystyle A(1,0,1,1,2,0,1;\nu^{\prime})B_{0}(1,1,2,0,0,0;2,2;\nu^{\prime})+A(1,2,1,1,0,0,1;\nu^{\prime})B_{0}(1,1,0,0,0,0;2,0;\nu^{\prime})

\displaystyle=A(1,0,1,1,0,0,1;\nu^{\prime})B_{0}(1,1,0,0,0,0;2,2;\nu^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times(2\pi)^{-2}\{(s_{2}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{1})(s_{2}-q+\nu_{2})+(s_{1}+\nu_{4})(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+\gamma_{1})\}

\displaystyle=A(1,0,1,1,0,0,1;\nu^{\prime})B_{0}(1,1,0,0,0,0;2,2;\nu^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\times(2\pi)^{-2}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\nu_{4})(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+\nu_{4}+\nu_{2})

\displaystyle=A(1,0,1,1,0,2,1;\nu^{\prime})B_{0}(1,1,0,0,0;2;\nu^{\prime}),

Lemma
6.11
with
\mu=\nu^{\prime}
leads us that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,1,1,1;\nu^{\prime})L_{2}(1,1,1,0,0,0;\nu^{\prime})=L_{1}(1,1,1,0;\nu)L_{2}(0,0,0,1,1,1;\nu).

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})=(4\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-2}R(4E_{1,2}E_{3,4}){\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{\bf 0})(\hat{y})=y_{1}y_{3}{\varphi}_{\sigma}(u_{\bf 0})(\hat{y}),

,

6.6.
Proof of Theorem
6.9

By Lemma
6.6
, we know that

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=2^{-2}\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl(m_{i}\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)\exp(-\sqrt{-1}b\,\theta_{2})\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}W(\hat{y})&\mbox{cases 2-(a),(c),(f)},\\
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}-1}\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{4})(\hat{y})&\mbox{case 2-(b)},\\
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}-1}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{p}})\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{1})(\hat{y})-R(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{p}})\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{3})(\hat{y})\}&\mbox{case 2-(d)},\\
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+1}\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{2,24}^{\prime})(\hat{y})&\mbox{case 2-(e)}.\end{cases}

Then Lemma
6.1
implies that

(6.3)

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(0,b)})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+b)V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2}).

Here we use the notation
\gamma_{1}=2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}.

Lemma 6.12
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, and

a_{i},b_{j},c_{k},d_{l}\in\mathbb{C}

(1\leq i,k,l\leq 5,1\leq j\leq 7)
,
we set

\displaystyle A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+a_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-2j+a_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j+a_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+a_{5}),

\displaystyle B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5};b_{6},b_{7})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+2j+b_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+b_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+b_{3})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}+b_{6})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j+b_{7})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2}+b_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3}+b_{5}),

\displaystyle C(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+c_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+c_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+c_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\kappa_{1}+c_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+c_{5}),

\displaystyle D_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4};d_{5})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+d_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+d_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{2}+d_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(q+\nu_{3}+d_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\kappa_{1}+d_{5})}

and

\displaystyle{B}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5};b_{6},b_{7})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5};b_{6},b_{7})\,dq,

\displaystyle{D}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4};d_{5})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}D_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4};d_{5})\,dq.

(i)
Let
\delta\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
\kappa_{1}-\delta\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
If
a_{3}+a_{4}+b_{1}+\delta=b_{7}
,
a_{3}+b_{1}+\delta=b_{3}
and
a_{3}+b_{1}=b_{6}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\delta}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\delta}{2}}{j}A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5})B(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4},b_{5};b_{6},b_{7})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{3}+a_{4}+\delta)}\cdot C(a_{2},a_{5},a_{4},a_{3}+a_{4},a_{3}+\delta)D(b_{1},b_{2},b_{4},b_{5};a_{3}+a_{4}+b_{1}).

(ii)
If
d_{5}=d_{1}+2d_{2}+d_{3}+d_{4}
,
c_{2}=d_{1}+d_{2}+d_{3}+d_{4}
and

c_{4}=2d_{2}+d_{3}+d_{4}
,
then we have

\displaystyle C(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5}){D}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4};d_{5})

\displaystyle=L_{1}(c_{1},d_{1}+d_{3},d_{1}+d_{4})L_{2}(d_{2}+d_{3},d_{2}+d_{4},c_{5},c_{3}),

where

\displaystyle L_{1}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\alpha_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2}+\alpha_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+\nu_{3}+\alpha_{3}),

\displaystyle L_{2}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\beta_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{3}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\beta_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\beta_{4}).

Proof.

The first claim (i) follows from Lemma
6.2
.
The latter is immediate from Lemma
1.2
together with the duplication formula
(
1.4
).
∎

Case 2-(a):
Since
W^{\prime}(\hat{y})=W(\hat{y})
can be written as

\displaystyle(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}\varphi_{\sigma}\bigl(\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}((\xi_{1})^{2}+(\xi_{3})^{2})^{\kappa_{1}/2}\bigr)(y)=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}{j}\varphi_{\sigma}\bigl(\mathrm{q}_{\mathcal{R}}((\xi_{1})^{j}(\xi_{3})^{\kappa_{1}-2j})\bigr)(y),

we have

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}\!\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}{j}(\sqrt{-1})^{-2j+\kappa_{1}-2j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j)e_{3}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}{j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j)e_{3}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}).

Then (
6.3
), Theorem
5.15
(i) and
6.12
imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}{j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j)e_{3}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,0,0,0)B(0,0,0,0,0;0,0)

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,0,0)D(0,0,0,0;0)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,0,0)L_{2}(0,0,0,0).

Case 2-(c):
As is the case 1-(c), we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(2E_{1,2})\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{3,4})(\hat{y})-\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{3,4})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+R(2E_{3,4})\varphi_{\sigma}(w_{1,2})(\hat{y})-\varphi_{\sigma}(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{1,2})(\hat{y})

Since we can see that

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}{j}(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)u_{(2j+1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-3)e_{3}+e_{4}},

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}{j}\{\sqrt{-1}y_{1}\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)e_{3}+e_{4}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{-1}y_{2}\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{(2j+1)e_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}+e_{4}})(\hat{y})+\sqrt{-1}y_{3}\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{(2j+1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\qquad+(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)\varphi_{\sigma}(u_{(2j+1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-3)e_{3}+e_{4}})(\hat{y})\},

that is,

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}{j}\{V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)e_{3}+e_{4}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+V_{\sigma,(2j+1)e_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}+e_{4}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{3})+V_{\sigma,(2j+1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}+1)

\displaystyle\qquad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)V_{\sigma,(2j+1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-3)e_{3}+e_{4}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\}.

6.3
) and Theorem

(i) imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}{j}\{A(0,1,0,0,0)B(1,0,1,0,0;1,1)

\displaystyle\quad+A(0,0,0,2,0)B(1,1,1,0,0;1,3)+A(0,0,-2,2,1)B(1,0,1,0,0;-1,3)

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)A(0,0,-2,2,0)B(1,0,1,0,0;-1,3)\}.

In view of

\displaystyle A(0,1,0,0,0)B_{0}(1,0,1,0,0;1,1)+A(0,0,0,2,0)B_{0}(1,1,1,0,0;1,3)

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)A(0,0,-2,2,0)B_{0}(1,0,1,0,0;-1,3)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-1)\}

\displaystyle=A(0,0,-2,0,0)B_{0}(1,0,1,0,0;1,3)\cdot{(2\pi)^{-3}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\{(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+1)(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}^{2}j-2)

\displaystyle\qquad+(s_{2}+2\nu_{1})(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-1)\}

\displaystyle=A(0,1,0,0,0)B_{0}(1,0,3,0,0;1,3)+(2\pi)^{-1}(s_{1}+2\nu_{1})\,A(0,0,-2,2,0)B_{0}(1,0,1,0,0;-1,3),

Case 2-(d):
As is the case 1-(d), we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}-1}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(2E_{2,3})\varphi(w_{1})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{1})(\hat{y})

that is,

\displaystyle\quad+V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j)e_{3}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{3})-(2\pi)^{-1}(2j)V_{\sigma,(2j-1)e_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j)e_{3}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\}.

Then (
6.3
) and Theorem

(i) imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}}{j}\{A(1,0,0,2,0)B(1,1,0,0,0;1,2)

\displaystyle\quad+A(1,1,0,0,0)B(1,0,0,0,0;1,0)-(2\pi)^{-1}(2j)A(1,0,0,0,0)B(-1,0,0,0,0;-1,0)\}.

In view of

\displaystyle A(1,0,0,2,0)B_{0}(1,1,0,0,0;1,2)+A(1,1,0,0,0)B_{0}(1,0,0,0,0;1,0)

\displaystyle-(2\pi)^{-1}(2j)A(1,0,0,0,0)B_{0}(-1,0,0,0,0;-1,0)

\displaystyle=A(1,0,0,0,0)B_{0}(-1,0,0,0,0;1,2)\cdot{(2\pi)^{-3}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\{(s_{2}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)(s_{1}-q+2j-1)(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})(s_{1}-q+2j-1)(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)

\displaystyle\qquad-2j(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-1)(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)\}

\displaystyle=A(1,0,0,0,0)B_{0}(-1,0,0,0,0;1,2)\cdot{(2\pi)^{-3}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\{(s_{1}-q-1)(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-1)(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+2j)

\displaystyle\qquad-(s_{1}-q+2j-1)(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1})\}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}(s_{1}-q-1)\,A(1,0,0,0,0)B_{0}(-1,0,0,0,0;-1,0)-A(1,0,0,0,0)B_{0}(1,1,2,0,0;1,2),

we can use Lemma
6.12
(i) to get

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)=C(0,0,0,0,1)\{{D}(1,0,0,0;-1)-{D}(1,1,0,0;1)\}.

Since

\displaystyle C(0,0,0,0,1)\{D_{0}(1,0,0,0;-1)-D_{0}(1,1,0,0;1)\}

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,0,1)D_{0}(1,0,0,0;1)\cdot(2\pi)^{-1}\{(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\nu_{3}+\kappa_{1}-1)-(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2})\}

\displaystyle=C(1,0,0,0,1)D_{0}(1,0,0,0;1),

Lemma
6.12
(ii) leads us that

Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)=L_{1}(0,1,1)L_{2}(0,0,1,0).

Case 2-(e):
We know

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-2}{2}}{j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}+e_{24}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}).

Case 2-(f):
As is the case 1-(c), we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(2E_{1,2})\varphi(w_{12})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{12})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad-R(2E_{2,3})\varphi(w_{23})(\hat{y})+\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{23})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{3,4})\varphi(w_{34})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{34})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{1,4})\varphi(w_{14})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{14})(\hat{y})\}.

Since

\displaystyle-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{12}+\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{23}-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{34}-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},0,1)}(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{14}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)u_{2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}+e_{4}+e_{23}}+2ju_{(2j-1)e_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)e_{3}+e_{4}+e_{14}}\},

the relations

\displaystyle\quad+V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)e_{3}+e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{3})+V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)e_{3}+e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}+1)

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-2j-1)V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-2j-2)e_{3}+e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\},

and (

), Theorem

in

.

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,1,0)L_{2}(0,0,1,1).

The cases 2-(d) and (f) can be similarly done.

6.7.
Proof of Theorem
6.10

By Lemma
6.7
, we know that

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=2^{-2}\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\int_{0}^{2\pi}W\bigl(m_{i}\,\hat{y}\,\tilde{\iota}(\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{1}}^{(2)},\mathrm{k}_{\theta_{2}}^{(2)})\bigr)\exp(-\sqrt{-1}b\,\theta_{2})\,\frac{d\theta_{1}}{2\pi}\frac{d\theta_{2}}{2\pi}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}W(\hat{y})&\mbox{cases 3-(a), (b)},\\
(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}\varphi_{\sigma}(\delta_{2}w_{2}+\delta_{1}w_{4})(\hat{y})&\mbox{case 3-(c)}.\end{cases}

Then Lemma
6.1
implies that

(6.4)

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi_{(0,b)})=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+b)V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{1}+s_{2}).

Here we use the notation
\gamma_{1}=2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}.

Lemma 6.13
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
, and

a_{i},b_{j},c_{k},d_{l}\in\mathbb{C}

(1\leq i,k\leq 7,1\leq j\leq 6,1\leq l\leq 4)
, we set

\displaystyle A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6};a_{7})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+a_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j+a_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+2j+a_{4})

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+a_{5})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-1+a_{6})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}-1+a_{7})},

\displaystyle B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4};b_{5},b_{6})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+2j+b_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-\kappa_{2}+b_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+b_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}+b_{4})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}+b_{5})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+2j+b_{6})},

\displaystyle C(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5},c_{6};c_{7})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+c_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+s_{2}+\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+c_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}+c_{3})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+c_{4})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+c_{5})\cdot\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2}-1+c_{6})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}-1+c_{7})},

\displaystyle D_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3};d_{4})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}-q+d_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}-q+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}-\kappa_{2}+d_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(q+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{2}-1}{2}+d_{3})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{1}+2s_{2}-q+2\nu_{1}+2\nu_{2}+\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}+d_{4})}

and

\displaystyle{B}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4};b_{5},b_{6})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}B_{0}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4};b_{5},b_{6})\,dq,

\displaystyle{D}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3};d_{4})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{-1}}\int_{q}D_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3};d_{4})\,dq.

(i) Let
\delta\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-\delta\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
If
a_{3}+a_{4}+b_{1}+\delta=b_{6}
,
a_{3}+b_{1}+\delta=b_{3}
and
a_{3}+b_{1}=b_{5}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-\delta}{2}}\binom{\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-\delta}{2}}{j}A(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6};a_{7})B(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4};b_{5},b_{6})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+a_{3}+a_{4}+\delta)}\cdot C(a_{2},a_{5},a_{3}+a_{4},a_{3}+\delta,a_{4},a_{6};a_{7})D(b_{1},b_{2},b_{4};a_{3}+a_{4}+b_{1}).

(ii)
If
d_{4}=d_{1}+2d_{2}+2d_{3}
,
c_{2}=d_{1}+d_{2}+2d_{3}
,
c_{3}=2(d_{2}+d_{3})
and
c_{7}=d_{2}+d_{3}
,
then we have

\displaystyle C(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5},c_{6},c_{7}){D}(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3};d_{4})

\displaystyle=L_{1}(c_{1},d_{1}+d_{3})L_{2}(d_{2}+d_{3},c_{6},c_{4},c_{5}).

where

\displaystyle L_{1}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{1}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\alpha_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-1}{2}+\alpha_{2}),

\displaystyle L_{2}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}+\beta_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s_{2}+\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}-2}{2}+\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\times\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}+\beta_{3})\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s_{2}+2\nu_{2}+\beta_{4}).

Proof.

As is Lemma
6.12
, our claim follows from Lemmas
1.2
and
6.2
.
∎

Case 3-(a):
We know

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+\kappa_{2}e_{24}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}).

), Theorem

and Lemma

imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,0,0,0;0)D(0,0,0;0)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,0)L_{2}(0,0,0,0).

Case 3-(b):
As is the case 2-(f), we know

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(\hat{y})

\displaystyle=(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}}(4\pi)^{-1}\{R(2E_{1,2})\varphi(w_{12})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{12})(y)

\displaystyle\quad-R(2E_{2,3})\varphi(w_{23})(\hat{y})+\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{23})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{3,4})\varphi(w_{34})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{34})(\hat{y})

\displaystyle\quad+R(2E_{1,4})\varphi(w_{14})(\hat{y})-\varphi(\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{14})(\hat{y})\}.

Since

\displaystyle-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,2}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{12}+\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{2,3}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{23}-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{3,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{34}-\tau_{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},0)}(E_{1,4}^{\mathfrak{k}})w_{14}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}\{2(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)u_{2je_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j-1)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-1)e_{24}+e_{23}}

\displaystyle\qquad-2(\kappa_{2}-1)u_{2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-2)e_{24}+e_{23}+e_{34}}\},

we get

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}\{V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-1)e_{24}+e_{12}}(s_{1}+1,s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle\quad+V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-1)e_{24}+e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2}+1,s_{3})

\displaystyle\quad+V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-1)e_{24}+e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}+1)

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j-1)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-1)e_{24}+e_{23}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}(\kappa_{2}-1)V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j)e_{3}+(\kappa_{2}-2)e_{24}+e_{23}+e_{34}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\}.

Then (
6.4
) and Theorem
5.16
imply that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=Z_{1}+Z_{2,1}+Z_{2,2}+Z_{3}+Z_{4,1}+Z_{4,2}+Z_{5,1}+Z_{5,2},

where

\displaystyle Z_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,1,1,1,0,0;1){B}(1,1,0,0;2,1),

\displaystyle Z_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,1,1,0,1;2){B}(0,2,1,0;1,2),

\displaystyle Z_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,1,1,0,1;2){B}(1,2,0,0;2,1),

\displaystyle Z_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,1,1,1,0;1){B}(0,1,1,0;1,2),

\displaystyle Z_{4,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2\pi}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2}{2}}{j}A(0,0,-1,1,0,0;1){B}(0,1,1,0;-1,2),

\displaystyle Z_{4,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2\pi}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2}{2}}{j}A(0,0,-1,1,0,0;1){B}(1,1,0,0;0,1),

\displaystyle Z_{5,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\kappa_{2}+1}{2\pi}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,1,1,0,0;2){B}(0,2,1,0;1,2),

\displaystyle Z_{5,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\kappa_{2}+1}{2\pi}\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}A(0,0,1,1,0,0;2){B}(1,2,0,0;2,1).

Here we used

(-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+2j)\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}}{2}}{j}=(-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2})\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2}{2}}{j}

in
Z_{4,1}
and
Z_{4,2}
.
Then Lemma
6.13
(i) implies that

\displaystyle Z_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+2)}\cdot C(0,0,2,1,1,1;2){D}(0,2,1;2),

\displaystyle Z_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+2)}\cdot C(0,1,2,1,1,0;1){D}(0,1,0;2),

\displaystyle Z_{4,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+2)}\cdot\frac{-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}{2\pi}\cdot C(0,0,0,1,1,0;1){D}(0,1,0;0),

\displaystyle Z_{5,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}+\gamma_{1}+2)}\cdot\frac{-\kappa_{2}+1}{2\pi}\cdot C(0,0,2,1,1,0;2){D}(0,2,1;2).

As is the case 2-(f) we can see that

\displaystyle Z_{2,1}+Z_{3}+Z_{4,1}+Z_{5,1}

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,1,1,0;1){D}(0,1,0;0).

For the sum
Z_{1}+Z_{2,2}+Z_{3}+Z_{4,2}+Z_{5,2}
, in view of

\displaystyle A(0,1,1,1,0,0;1)B(1,1,0,0;2,1)+A(0,0,1,1,0,1;2)B(1,2,0,0;2,1)

\displaystyle+(2\pi)^{-1}(-\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}+2j)A(0,0,-1,1,0,0;1)B(1,1,0,0;0,1)

\displaystyle+(2\pi)^{-1}(-\kappa_{2}+1)A(0,0,1,1,0,0;2)B(0,2,1,0;1,2)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}-1)A(0,0,-1,1,0,0;1)B(1,1,0,0;0,1),

we can use Lemma
6.13
(i) to get

\displaystyle Z_{1}+Z_{2,2}+Z_{4,2}+Z_{5,2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-1}(s_{2}+2\nu_{1}-1)C(0,0,0,-1,1,0;1){D}(1,1,0;1)

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,1,1,0;1){D}(0,0,1;0).

Here we substituted
q\to q+1
.
Therefore Lemma
6.13
(ii) implies that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,1,1,0;1)\{{D}(0,1,0;0)+{D}(0,0,1;0)\}

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,1,1,0;0){D}(0,0,0;0)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,0)L_{2}(0,0,1,1).

Case 3-(c):
We know

\displaystyle V^{\prime}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}{j}V_{\sigma,2je_{1}+\delta_{2}e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j-1)e_{3}+\delta_{1}e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{24}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3}).

Then (
6.4
) and Theorem
5.16
and Lemma
6.13
lead us that

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},W,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}{j}A(1,0,-\delta_{2},\delta_{2},0,0;0){B}(0,0,\delta_{1},0;-\delta_{2},1)

\displaystyle=C(0,0,0,\delta_{1},\delta_{2},0;0){D}(0,0,0;0)

\displaystyle=L_{1}(0,0)L_{2}(0,0,\delta_{1},\delta_{2}).

The evaluation of the contragredient zeta integral
Z(s_{1},s_{2},\widetilde{W},\widehat{\Phi})
can be
done in the same way as in the case 2. For example, in the case 3-(c), we have

\displaystyle Z(s_{1},s_{2},\widetilde{W},\widehat{\Phi})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2s_{2}-\gamma_{1})\cdot(-\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}}(\sqrt{-1})

\displaystyle\quad\times\sum_{0\leq j\leq\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}\binom{\frac{\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-1}{2}}{j}(\sqrt{-1})^{\delta_{2}-\delta_{1}-\kappa_{2}}(-1)^{\kappa_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j-1)}

\displaystyle\quad\times V_{\tilde{\sigma},2je_{1}+\delta_{2}e_{2}+(\kappa_{1}-\kappa_{2}-2j-1)e_{3}+\delta_{1}e_{4}+\kappa_{2}e_{24}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=(\sqrt{-1})^{\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{2\kappa_{1}+1}L(s_{1},\Pi_{\tilde{\sigma}})L(s_{2},\Pi_{\tilde{\sigma}},\wedge^{2})

as desired.
